Brazil biodiesel demand tightens soybean oil as crushing slows
Biodiesel peak strains Brazil oil supplies
Crushing margins prompt maintenance shutdowns
Argentina poised to capture displaced exports
Peak Brazilian biodiesel demand and a seasonal decline in soybean crushing are tightening domestic soybean oil supplies, supporting prices but weakening Brazil's export competitiveness after a sharp increase in shipments during 2026, according to StoneX.
Brazilian biodiesel sales typically peak during the fifth twomonth period of the year, covering September and October, which increases demand for soybean oil during the domestic soybean off-season, StoneX said in its fourth-quarter commodities outlook Oct. 20.
At the same time, weaker crushing margins have prompted some processors to bring maintenance stoppages forward.
Brazilian soybean crushing increased 7.4% year over year during January-July, according to data from the industry association Abiove cited in the report.
Domestic soybean oil demand remained broadly stable over the same period.
However, the seasonal reduction in processing has since limited oil availability just as biodiesel demand approaches its annual peak.
Soybean oil values at Paranaguá rose nearly 7% in the third quarter, while the premium over Chicago narrowed to 11 cents/ pound at the end of September, down from 20 cents/lb in midJune, according to StoneX.
The tighter balance comes after a particularly strong period for exports.
Brazil shipped an estimated 1.57 million metric tons of soybean oil during January-August, up 41% year over year and already above the total exported in all of 2025.
Higher domestic prices are now expected to reduce the competitiveness of Brazilian soybean oil in overseas markets and slow shipments in the final quarter.
This shift could create additional opportunities for Argentina, the world's largest soybean oil exporter.
Argentine soybean crushing remained strong during recent months, increasing 8.8% year over year, and the country is expected to maintain ample soybean oil availability despite a seasonal slowdown in processing, StoneX said.
Competitive crushing margins and continued Asian demand should allow Argentine oil to capture business displaced by the seasonal tightening in Brazil.
Black Sea logistics add another layer to the vegetable oil trade. Russia and Ukraine account for more than 60% of the global sunflower oil trade, but repeated attacks on processing and port infrastructure have disrupted shipments.
Approximately 150,000 mt of sunflower oil cargoes scheduled for August and September were postponed, while Indian

October 21, 2026

sunflower oil imports fell to a six-month low in August, according to the report.
Those disruptions could support demand for South American soybean oil despite a growing US crush supply and comfortable palm oil inventories.
For Brazil, the near-term soybean oil outlook will hinge on how quickly crushing recovers amid sustained biodiesel requirements. Prices could remain supported during the off-season, but higher domestic values are likely to shift a larger share of export demand to Argentina, the firm said.

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Gasoil Daily Market Analysis
South Korea reject refiners' call for higher diesel exports
High freight rates limit arbitrage flows despite tight European market
Backwardation in the Asian ultra-low sulfur gasoil market narrowed on Oct. 21, capped by South Korea maintaining its cap on refined product exports.
Brokers pegged the front-month November-December Singapore gasoil swaps time spread at $3.18/b in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 21, narrowing from $3.40/b at the 0830 GMT Asian close Oct. 20.
On physical cargoes, the benchmark Platts-assessed cash differential for FOB Singapore gasoil with a maximum sulfur content of 10 parts per million rose 32 cents/b day over day to $1.15/b at the 0830 GMT Asian close Oct. 20.
The South Korean government is holding firm on ensuring adequate domestic supplies of clean oil products by capping exports at 2025 levels, rejecting the refining sector's proposal to increase overseas sales, Platts earlier reported.
The government imposed restrictions on exports of gasoline, diesel, and jet fuel/kerosene on March 13, stating refiners cannot export more of the controlled products in any month than they did in the corresponding month of 2025. They must also maintain monthly domestic supplies at more than 90% of the same month's sales volume a year earlier, according to the country's Ministry of Trade, Industry and Resources.
Still, the ceiling is not entirely inflexible. The government may approve exports exceeding 100% of year-earlier volumes after consulting refiners, provided market and domestic inventory conditions permit, MOTIR said.
"Directionally, it will be lifted, but I guess it will take a while," an Asia-based middle distillates trader said.
A surge in clean tanker rates has led Asia-based refiners to be unable to capitalize on the extremely tight diesel market in Europe.

The front-month November gasoil EFS -- the spread between Singapore 10 ppm sulfur gasoil swaps and the corresponding ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract -- widened to minus $164.34/mt on Oct. 20, compared to the September average of minus $148.39/ mt, Platts data showed. A steeply discounted spread typically indicates more economically lucrative arbitrage economics.
In statistical news, oil product stockpiles at the UAE's Fujairah rebounded 6.2% in the week ended Oct. 19, led by a record 72% jump in light distillates such as gasoline and naphtha, according to Fujairah Oil Industry Zone data published Oct. 21.
Middle distillate inventories declined 18% to 2.261 million barrels after hitting their highest level since the start of the war a week earlier.
Elsewhere, Russian diesel and gasoil exports more than doubled to 152,000 mt in the week ended Oct. 15, up from 39,00 mt the previous week after the government partially lifted the export ban, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.
Total loadings of Russian diesel and gasoil over Oct. 1-20 reached 375,000 mt, compared with roughly 453,000 mt in September.
In comparison, loadings were about 2.3 million mt in September 2025, while in October of that year they reached nearly 2.7 million mt.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Mei huey Ng

Platts European Naphtha Daily Market Analysis
Naphtha prices drop as LPG gains edge
Gasoline complex slows day over day
The European naphtha complex was weaker Oct. 21 with the crack spread, the front backwardation and the physical price falling day over day.
The European gasoline-naphtha spread has been weakening as rising LPG prices improved naphtha's competitiveness as a petrochemical feedstock, while weak arbitrage economics limited gasoline export opportunities.
As gasoline blending demand for naphtha waned, petrochemical buyers stepped back into the market, with rising LPG prices improving naphtha's competitiveness as an alternative feedstock.
Propane prices have risen amid sluggish US exports and slow production in the North Sea, widening the propane-naphtha spread and making LPG less competitive as a feedstock for flexible steam crackers.
Meanwhile, a softer octane complex has weakened reforming margins, weighing on demand for heavy virgin naphtha.

Gas Liquids
US DATA: Propane, propylene stocks fall as exports rise above 2 mil b/d
Inventory decline lesser than expected
Production holds steady at around 2.9 mil b/d
US propane and propylene inventories fell below 106 million barrels for the first time since early August during the week ended Oct. 16, while exports rose and overall demand fell short of analyst projections, according to US Energy Information Administration supply and demand data published Oct. 21.
Stocks tightened by 500,000 barrels to 105.846 million barrels, their lowest level since early August, when stocks hovered at 104.974 million barrels. The decline marks the third consecutive weekly stock draw, the first three-week span of inventory tightening since late January to early February.
The US Gulf Coast accounted for nearly the entire stock draw, with regional stocks down 789,000 barrels to 65.939 million barrels. Other regions built inventories, including PADD 3, which built 398,000 b/d and PADD 1, which built 91,000 b/d.
Total demand, including exports and domestic product supplied, fell 149,000 barrels/day to 3.052 million b/d, according to EIA data. Of that total, exports rose just 40,000 b/d to 2.032 million b/d. Meanwhile, domestic product supplied fell 189,000 b/d to 1.020 million b/d.
The stock draw was lower than expected by S&P Global Energy CERA analysts, who projected a steeper 1.252-millionbarrel stock draw based on the assumption that inventory draws

would begin to accelerate in October. "The demand trend in fall remains very strong compared with
2025, but weekly demand moves may be volatile," CERA analysts said in their Oct. 19 propane market forecast.
S&P Global Commodities at Sea shipping data showed a week-over-week export increase, though slightly higher at 2.1 million b/d for the week ended Oct. 16, versus the 2.032 million b/d in the EIA report for the same period.
During that week, Japan was by far the largest destination for US-loaded propane barrels, at 42% of all loadings, followed by China, at 12%.
Meanwhile, production volumes fell slightly to 2.889 million b/d, though have held steady over the last two months, with average propane and propylene production at 2.931 million b/d since the first week of September.
Amid the inventory drawdown, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, heard US Gulf Coast propane at Mont Belvieu's Enterprise terminal during morning down 1.25 cents/gallon to $1.0925/gal on Oct. 21.
-- Hunter Marrow

European propane physical trading remained subdued Oct. 21 despite tight prompt availability, with supply constraints and expectations of higher winter consumption pointing to a tighter balance into December.
The subdued activity concerned the physical market, "not the paper market," a market source said.
Restricted North Sea production continued to limit regional availability, while rising eastbound US exports were expected to further constrain supply to Europe as seasonal demand increased.
US export availability remained comfortable, although weaker Far East buying interest narrowed arbitrage opportunities. Chinese importers continued to favor non-US supply, directing more US-origin barrels toward other Asian markets.
Asian demand remained uneven across delivery periods. An anticipated seasonal increase in North Asian consumption could intensify competition for available cargoes as winter progresses.
In the CIF large cargo market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the Platts Market on Close assessment process. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.

On demand, the switch to winter-grade gasoline specifications had lifted blender pull for butane as a vapor-pressure and octane component. Retail LPG demand was also beginning its seasonal climb into the heating season, adding to prompt interest across the region.
Supply remained the dominant theme, with LPG shipments through the Strait of Hormuz expected to fall sharply in October following renewed attacks on commercial vessels.
This tightens East of Suez balances and keeps Asian buyers competing hard for US Gulf Coast cargoes. A firmer US propane market had also narrowed the transatlantic arbitrage, limiting the flow of long-haul tons into Europe.
Freight offered little relief. VLGC earnings remained historically elevated, although rates had started to ease from their peaks. High freight continued to favor short-haul North Sea and Mediterranean coaster supply over long-haul imports.
Structurally, the EU ban on Russian butane and isobutane has forced Poland, previously the lead buyer of Russian supply, to source alternative barrels. That continues to draw NWE product east toward Central and Eastern Europe and tighten availability around the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp hub, leaving the coaster market with limited spare supply heading into winter.
In the butane CIF NWE large cargo market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the Platts Market on Close assessment process. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the CIF large cargo market in outright terms at $1,007.25/mt, down $12.25/mt day over day and stable as a percentage to naphtha at 110.75%.
In the absence of indications in the MOC, the butane coaster market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the FOB Seagoing butane coaster market at $1,128/mt, down $14/mt day over day and stable versus naphtha at 124%.
It assessed the CIF Seagoing market at $1,091/mt, down $14/mt day over day and stable as a percentage versus naphtha at 120%.
This put the FOB coasters at a $37/mt premium to the CIF coaster market.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Baran Serdaroglu

West Med LPG market stays tight on supply
Algerian cargoes divert to India from region
Butane trades at $1,155/mt, down $14 daily

African buying outweighing softer crude and naphtha signals, while butane continued to hold a premium to propane on firm blending and bottled-gas demand.
Algerian contract prices remained the regional anchor after a steep increase for October for both propane and butane. Attention was now turning to November contract prices, due around monthend, as a signal for winter term pricing across the Mediterranean.
Algerian supply was being spread more widely than usual. Algerian LPG has been moving to India in recent months as Asian buyers replace lost Gulf barrels. This has reduced the volume of North African cargoes available for prompt Mediterranean coaster trade.

The East Med and Turkey were also drawing on West Med cargoes as Gulf export disruptions persisted, with flows through Hormuz expected to slump this month.
Energy costs added further support. European natural gas prices have climbed steeply over the past year, keeping LPG competitive for industrial and heating demand and supporting distributor buying ahead of the colder months.

crisis in the waterway, Iranian foreign minister Abbas Araghchi said in an Oct. 20 interview with Iraqi state news.
In March, Iraq said the country was in talks with Iran to allow some tankers carrying Iraqi crude to pass out of the Persian Gulf through Hormuz.
Iraq's state oil marketer SOMO and the oil ministry declined to comment on the matter Oct. 21.
"Tehran is aware of the extent of the losses and economic burdens that Iraq has incurred as a result of the developments in the strait," Araghchi said. "The Iranian side has made efforts to mitigate these costs ... [including] opening special routes for Iraqi ships to pass through the Strait of Hormuz."
The comments come as a tanker was "struck by an unknown projectile" 30 nautical miles off the coast of Iraq's port city Al Faw on Oct. 20, according to the UK Maritime Trade Operations.
UKMTO said there was no reported damage or environmental impact and advised vessels to transit with caution.
So far in October, Iraq's crude exports have averaged 3.4 million barrels/day through the Strait of Hormuz, according to comments from Iraqi oil minister Bassem Mohammed Khudair carried by state news INA Oct. 20.
S&P Global Commodities at Sea data shows that in the first 10 days of October, Iraq was loading around 3.06 million b/d of crude, but that rate has slowed in the last 10 days to 690,900 b/d. Loadings at ports inside the Persian Gulf have slowed recently as attacks on ships mount. Ship tracking data may also fail to capture the true number of loadings at ports due to high volumes of "dark loadings."
Iraq was hit particularly hard when regional war broke out at the end of February and the Strait of Hormuz was effectively shut. Iraq, heavily reliant on oil revenues to fund its state coffers, had to quickly shut in oil production with export routes blocked and only limited storage options.
Iraq's crude production has since recovered, pumping 3.9 million b/d in September, according to the latest OPEC+ survey from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy. The month before the war, Iraq produced 4.22 million b/d.
The country relies heavily on its southern terminals at Basrah, with a 3.5 million b/d capacity, to export its Basrah Medium and Basrah Heavy crude grades, but the crisis in the Strait of Hormuz saw export levels plummet to just 523,000 b/d in June. Export

volumes have recovered since then, climbing to 2.78 million b/d in September, CAS data shows.
The data shows exports of oil products have also been severely affected by the war.
Ongoing negotiations In the interview, Iran's Araghchi said negotiations with the US
to reopen the Strait of Hormuz and end the war were ongoing through Qatari mediators.
Iran has been adamant that it will maintain control over the Strait of Hormuz and has demanded that for a deal, the US must lift its maritime blockade, ease sanctions and restrictions on Iran's oil exports, release frozen assets, and end hostilities on all fronts -- prospects the US has largely opposed.
US Secretary of State Marco Rubio told reporters Oct. 20 that "any decision on what would be acceptable is in the hands of the President of the United States," according to a State Department readout.
"The United States will not support any deal that creates a pathway for Iran to ever have a nuclear weapon," he said.

CAS data shows weekly crossings of oil and LPG tankers reached 143 in the week ended Oct. 15, having amounted to the same level in the week ended Sept. 24, and 146 in the week ended Sept. 10, having averaged 106 year to date in 2026.
The crossings seen in recent weeks were the highest since December 2023, when weekly transits started to collapse from a normal level of 150-160 as Houthi militants launched intense attacks on merchant ships in the Red Sea as part of the IsraelHamas war.
Having ramped down their maritime attacks in 2024, the Yemen-based Houthi group declared a maritime blockade on all Saudi-linked shipping on July 20 and have since then targeted some Bahri tankers while refraining from targeting Western ships.
Suez traffic increase "closely coincides with escalating Houthi attacks targeting Saudi shipping near the Bab al-Mandab strait," CAS analysts said in an Oct. 20 report.
Overall, northbound crude flows via the Egyptian canal averaged 944,000 barrels per day in August, 949,000 b/d in September, and 924,000 b/d in October, to date, rising considerably higher than an average of 60,000 b/d between March and May, CAS analysts said, attributing the increase to a boost in Saudi crude flows.
Saudi Arabia has increased exports from its Red Sea coast since the outbreak of the Middle East war at the end of February, with heightened security risks around the Strait of Hormuz. Latest Houthi threats have prompted more Saudi shipments via Suez rather than Bab al-Mandab, according to CAS.
Saudi crude shipments moving north through the Suez reached about 653,000 b/d Oct. 1-19, according to CAS data, accounting for the majority of northbound crude flows. Red Seaorigin crude makes up more than 70% of northbound volumes since July, while the remaining 30% originates from the Middle East Gulf, CAS analysts said.

East of Suez Long Range tankers were higher Oct. 21 due to limited tankers prepared to go and load inside the Strait of Hormuz, but were lower outside the risky waters amid ample supply, market participants said.
There are around 45 LR1s and 28 LR2s expected to be available for loading in Oman and West Coast India in the next three weeks, according to shipping industry estimates. The LR fleet is coming back for loading their next cargo in the Middle East and India, said a chartering executive with a global commodities trading company.
Several brokers said that demand is slowing down, though there may be some outstanding cargoes that are yet to be covered, while no fresh cargoes were reported on the market. Supply is easing across both LR1s and LR2s, and with a lack of cargoes, freight outside the Strait of Hormuz is expected to soften, a clean tankers' broker said.

availability for prompt loading is tight, several MR brokers said. Some tankers were reported to be ballasting from Australia
towards Southeast Asia and North Asia, an MR broker said. Among outstanding cargoes, Pertamina is seeking an MR for Oct. 29 loading on the Singapore-Tuban route.
The Mona was placed on subjects at w670 by BP, for Oct. 30 diesel loading on the Quanzhou-Australia route, sources said.

News
US Treasury sanctions law firm for aiding Iranian oil trade
Law firm set up front companies
Shell companies traded Iranian oil and LPG
The US Treasury Department sanctioned a law firm based in Panama and Greece for allegedly facilitating illicit Iranian shipping activity, including setting up front companies and corporate architecture for companies that shipped Iranian oil and LPG.
Treasury also sanctioned several companies and associated vessels linked to shipping Iranian crude and petrochemicals, according to an Oct. 21 statement by Treasury's Office of Foreign Assets Control.
"Whether you are conducting illegal oil transfers at sea or sitting in the corner office of a law firm, if you are supporting the Iranian regime, we will find you and sanction you," Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said in the statement.
The sanctions are the latest in a regular cadence of new sanctions under the Trump administration's Operation Economic Outcast regime that seeks to pressure Iran to come to the negotiating table.
Treasury sanctioned the law firm Karnakis and Karnakis for its alleged role in setting up shell and front companies to help trade sanctioned Iranian products, according to the statement.
The firm registered shipping companies that own shadowfleet vessels responsible for shipping hundreds of millions of dollars' worth of Iranian steel, petroleum and petrochemical products, the statement said.
The firm did not immediately respond to a request for comment. The firm set up previously sanctioned companies, including Elias Shipping and Trading SA, which served as a front company to sell millions of barrels of Iranian oil for the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps-Qods Force and the Houthi rebels in Yemen, OFAC alleged. The firm also played a role in establishing the corporate architecture for the shipping empire of Iranian LPG magnate Seyed Asadoollah Emamjomeh, who was sanctioned in 2025. Treasury sanctioned the owners of the law firm, Sofianos Karnakis Kosmas and Konstantinos Karnakis Kosmas, as well as several employees, according to the statement. The sanctions also target the company Roxette Maritime Shipping and Trading and seven cargo ships it operates for moving Iranian petrochemicals and other products like steel, the statement said. The vessels are the Anemos, Gloris, Pride, Destiny, Flamingo 1, Harmony and Prince. Treasury sanctioned Amigoz Singapore PTE. Ltd., which acted as an agent for a shipment of light crude oil, OFAC said. The sanctions also target Whales Shipping Line LLC and two of the company's small support vessels. Whales acted as an agent for a company that organized the purchase of millions of dollars' worth of Iranian petrochemicals, according to the statement.
-- Kate Winston

India could deepen energy ties with Venezuela beyond crude imports: Rubio
Scope to expand India's upstream investments in Venezuela
India, US target over $500B in bilateral trade in three years
US crude can help India diversify energy sources: Rubio
Indian companies could play a larger role in Venezuela's energy sector as the country's oil production increases, importing more of its heavy crude for processing while exploring new opportunities to invest in upstream projects, US Secretary of State Marco Rubio said Oct. 20.
Speaking ahead of a meeting with Reliance Industries Ltd.'s executives at the company's Jamnagar refining complex, Rubio said India's extensive refining capacity positions it well to process additional Venezuelan crude as production increases, according to a US State Department statement.
"They have the capability to refine heavy crude. They've done so in the past, and I think as more and more Venezuelan oil comes online, it's something they can do," Rubio said, referring to Indian refiners.
Rubio added that energy cooperation is both an economic and a national security issue. Refining capacity is essential for turning crude into usable energy, while reliable energy supplies are critical to economic stability.
India imported about 300,000 barrels/day of Venezuelan crude in 2019, but volumes have since declined to about 25 million barrels in 2024. In 2025, five VLCCs -- one per month -- discharged Venezuelan crude at the Port of Sikka, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.
However, volumes have grown steadily this year. A total of 13.6 million barrels of Venezuelan-origin crude, including intransit volumes, is expected to be discharged at Indian ports in October, after 10.2 million barrels were discharged in May, CAS data showed, highlighting a sharp near-term increase in flows. Completed inflows of Venezuelan crude to India have averaged 190,000 b/d so far this year, with 80% of the volume delivered to Sikka port.
Ties with Reliance, state refiners Benjamin Tang, head of liquid bulk at CAS, said Reliance is
the largest buyer of Venezuelan crude and is likely to remain central to any US effort to expand Venezuela-India oil trade, given its refinery configuration and ability to process heavier grades. However, further growth may be limited by India's relatively modest heavy-crude processing capacity, with only a small number of refineries equipped to handle grades such as Merey-16.
Rubio also highlighted the potential for India's role in Venezuela's energy sector to expand beyond crude imports. He said Indian companies have previously held oil concessions in Venezuela and could increase production there, creating

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

34

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 21, 2026

opportunities for investment in exploration and production, as well as refining.
"So, all these are positive things. We talked about it in the past. They're ready to do it, they're willing to do it," Rubio said.
Indian state-run oil companies entered Venezuela in 2008 to secure heavy crude resources and diversify their upstream portfolios. Oil and Natural Gas Corp.'s overseas arm, ONGC Videsh, holds a 40% participating interest in the San Cristobal project through its subsidiary ONGC San Cristobal BV, according to the company's website. The project is a joint venture with Venezuela's state-owned oil and gas company PDVSA, under Petrolera Indovenezolana SA.
In 2010, ONGC, along with Indian Oil Corp. and Oil India Ltd., participated in the Carabobo-1 project located in the Orinoco Belt. ONGC Videsh holds an 11% equity in the project, while IOC and Oil India hold a combined 7% stake through their 50-50 joint venture, IndOil Netherlands BV, according to the ONGC Videsh website and S&P Global Energy.
Rubio said discussions with Reliance would focus on understanding the company's current position and assessing potential crude oil production opportunities in Venezuela.
"Reliance may be willing to buy more Venezuelan crude, but experience of Indian national oil companies suggests that moving from crude buyer to upstream investor requires confidence not in the resource base, but in the durability of the aboveground framework," said Mansi Anand, principal analyst for research on national oil companies at S&P Global Energy CERA.
US oil can fill vacuum Rubio said New Delhi has made substantial progress in
diversifying its energy portfolio, although he acknowledged that shifting supply sources cannot happen immediately.
He added that a broader range of suppliers -- including Venezuela, the US and other producers -- will strengthen India's energy security in the future.
During a meeting in Mumbai with Indian Minister of External Affairs S. Jaishankar, Rubio said increased US-India energy trade could help diversify India's supply chains and meet its substantial consumer demand. The two sides also discussed broader cooperation in areas including defense, civil nuclear technology, semiconductors, critical minerals and energy, according to another US State Department statement Oct. 20.
The potential for expanded oil cooperation comes as the US and India pursue broader commercial ties. Bilateral trade has exceeded $240 billion, with the two countries aiming to raise that figure to more than $500 billion over the next three years, the statement said.
US crude oil flows to India averaged about 149,000 b/d in 2024, marked by a strong midyear surge followed by a secondhalf pullback. Flows were generally stronger but more uneven in 2025, averaging about 238,000 b/d, according to CAS data.

However, US crude flows to India weakened in 2026, averaging 128,000 b/d over January-October -- roughly 43% below the 2025 average for the same period, CAS data showed.
-- Sambit Mohanty, Ratnajyoti Dutta
FUJAIRAH DATA: Oil product stockpiles rebound as light distillates jump record 72%
Fujairah inventory climbs 6.2% to 7.247 mil barrels Light distillates rebound from record low Heavy distillates rise 12% to two-week high
Oil product stockpiles at the UAE's Fujairah rebounded 6.2% in the week ended Oct. 19, led by a record 72% jump in light distillates such as gasoline and naphtha, according to Fujairah Oil Industry Zone data published Oct. 21.
Total inventory rose to 7.247 million barrels, marking the first gain in four weeks, the data showed. Stocks remain 65% below the Feb. 23 level before the onset of the Iran war, as disruptions to ship traffic through the Strait of Hormuz continue. Fujairah is located on the UAE's east coast, just outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Light distillate stocks recovered to 1.304 million barrels from a record low of 760,000 barrels a week earlier, according to FOIZ data. The inventory increase was the largest recorded in FOIZ data shared with S&P Global Energy since 2017.
Stocks of heavy distillates, used as ship fuel and for power generation, rose 12% to 3.682 million barrels, representing a two-week high, while middle distillate inventories declined 18% to 2.261 million barrels after hitting their highest level since the start of the war a week earlier.
Since the war began, heavy distillate stocks have dropped 53%, while middle distillate inventories have fallen 23% and light distillate stocks have declined 87%.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed Fujairahdelivered marine fuel with 0.5% sulfur at $982/metric ton on Oct. 20, the highest since Oct. 13, while Fujairah-delivered highsulfur fuel 380 CST was assessed at $739/mt, the lowest since Oct. 13.
-- Claudia Carpenter

Dangote buys 25% stake in Kenya's state-owned pipeline company
Nigerian refiner acquires stake in Kenyan oil products network
Pipeline transports 250,000 b/d from Mombasa
Second UAE cargo inbound for Lekki, Middle East crude in focus for Lamu
Africa's largest refiner, the Dangote Group, has purchased a 25% stake in Kenya's state-owned pipeline company as it eyes new distribution channels for the East African market, its founder Aliko Dangote announced on Oct. 21.
In an appearance at the FT Africa Summit in London, the Nigerian industrialist announced that he had secured the interest in the Kenya Pipeline Company with the approval of the country's President, William Ruto, on Oct. 20, allowing his business to use its network to distribute its oil products.
Dangote cited the investment as part of the company's strategy toward acquiring new storage and distribution assets, as the company plots its entry to East Africa with a second refinery development. His business currently operates a 700,000 b/d refinery in Lekki, Nigeria, and is advancing plans to launch a second site in Lamu, Kenya by 2030.
KPC, which offered 65% of its shares in an initial public offering in January, operates the region's most sophisticated and low-cost fuel distribution system, with a system spanning 1,300 km. The network has the capacity to transport roughly 250,000 b/d of oil products from the port of Mombasa to cities in the

country's northeast, where some fuel is subsequently loaded onto trucks to travel further inland.
The acquisition will see Dangote sit alongside the Kenyan government, with its 35% stake in the pipeline business, as well as Uganda's national oil company, UNOC, which sought to acquire a 20% interest as part of the public offering. Opening the business to new investors was intended to help Kenya unlock new spending for its infrastructure, which has struggled to keep pace with growing inland demand.
The existing pipeline network has been expanded twice in recent years, and was due for another upgrade under previous KPC plans. Additionally, UNOC has expressed interest in reviving long-delayed plans to extend the KPC network over the Kenya-Uganda border, which has previously been set by regulatory and financial hurdles.
Kenya and Uganda are both discussing stakes in Dangote's Lamu project, which the company has said could potentially process future crude supplies from both countries. Speaking at the FT Summit, however, Dangote noted that limits to the potential oil output from both countries, neither of which are current producers, will mean the refinery needs to take a broader approach to its feedstock sourcing, naming the Middle East as a potential future supplier.
In Nigeria, the company designed its Lekki facility to process the light sweet crude native to the country, but has increasingly sought to tilt its supply towards some heavier grades. Despite disruption from the Middle East conflict, the company took delivery of its first cargo of UAE crude in August, when it received 1.9 milion barrels of the country's Das Blend grade, while another 1 million barrels of Upper Zakum crude is inbound in October, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea.

Infrastructure sprint Dangote referenced the Kenyan pipeline deal as part of a
wider set of infrastructure investments being advanced by the group, which has targeted $50 billion of new spending as part of its Vision 2030 strategy. Alongside its Kenyan refinery project in Lamu, the company is also working on new pipeline links between Djibouti and Ethiopia, as well as Namibia, Zimbabwe and Zambia.
To support its access to African markets and beyond, the company is additionally building tank farms in Djibouti, Cameroon, and Namibia, among others, the group founder said, without providing further timelines on the company's myriad construction projects.
The East African expansion comes as part of a push to tap unserved inland markets that are growing fast due to population growth and economic development, Dangote said in London.
"When you look at the eastern part of Africa, they need more refining than the western part, because they are into a lot of mining, and it is difficult for them to get diesel. The only thing that isn't a mature market is aviation, for which there's now a lot of market out of Africa," he said.
Uganda currently consumes roughly 45,000 b/d of refined products, although the volume could reach 65,000 b/d by 2035, according to estimates from S&P Global Energy CERA analysts.

-- Kelly Norways
Weatherford starts Venezuela oilfield work as operators accelerate activity plans
Company receives purchase orders, expands workforce
Venezuela startup costs rise faster than anticipated
Substantial increase in activity seen over coming months
Weatherford International has begun executing oilfield services orders in Venezuela and is rapidly expanding its local workforce as international operators accelerate plans to restore crude production, management said Oct. 21.
The company has received purchase orders, started work and advanced negotiations with several international operators, marking a shift from preliminary discussions to commercial activity as Venezuela seeks to revive its oil industry, Girish Saligram, CEO, said during the company's Q3 earnings call.
The pace and scope of discussions with operators has increased significantly in recent weeks, giving Weatherford greater confidence that a substantial expansion in oilfield activity is approaching, Saligram said.
"Conversations have gone from what if, to how soon, to how fast," he said.
Weatherford's growing confidence is supported by increasingly concrete commercial discussions and greater visibility into contracts already being negotiated, Saligram said.
Weatherford's preparations come as Venezuela attempts to increase crude production from approximately 1.2 million b/d.
The company is focusing particularly on international operators that have signed agreements to expand their activities in Venezuela, Saligram said, without identifying individual customers.
Weatherford's earnings release confirms a 46-unit artificial-lift equipment contract for well completions in Venezuela.
Weatherford has also begun rebuilding its operating capacity in the country, including recruitment, facility refurbishment and equipment procurement.
"We are furiously hiring people in Venezuela," Saligram said, adding that the company's headcount had increased "substantially" in recent weeks.
The expansion involves bringing employees on board, refurbishing facilities and securing inventories needed to support anticipated demand, he said.
The expenditures are creating some initial pressure on profitability and working capital, although Weatherford said the impact is relatively modest.
Saligram described the spending as necessary preparation for what the company expects to be a significant increase in demand for oilfield services.
He said the company expects to provide greater visibility into its customers and commercial activities over the coming months.
-- Sheky Espejo

Weatherford sees improved Pemex payments, but collections remain a cash flow risk
Pemex receivables at Weatherford hit six-year low
Collections now arrive 'like clockwork'
Mexico's Pemex has substantially improved its payment performance with oilfield services company Weatherford International, reducing its share of the contractor's outstanding receivables to the lowest level in more than six years, management said Oct. 21, but collections remain a significant factor in the company's cash flow outlook.
Payments from Pemex have become increasingly regular over the past year, following measures introduced by the Mexican government to address the oil producer's financial outstanding obligations to suppliers, Weatherford executives said during the company's third-quarter earnings call Oct. 21.
"Collections have been really coming in like clockwork," said Anjuv Dhruv, CFO.
However, Weatherford cautioned that the timing of future payments remains an important source of uncertainty for its quarterly cash generation.
"From a Pemex standpoint, yes, very, very positive that Pemex, the percent of our total accounts receivable, is at the lowest it's been in the last six-plus years," the executive said, adding that "they still continue to be a large, large variable in our quarterly free cash flow number."
Weatherford expects to receive additional payments from Pemex during the fourth quarter and has incorporated those collections into its financial guidance.
The company did not disclose the amount Pemex currently owes, despite being asked by an analyst to quantify its outstanding receivables.
The improvement comes as Pemex continues to rely on government financial support to address its debt burden, meet obligations to contractors and sustain investment in exploration, production and refining.
Pemex's payment difficulties have been a persistent concern for oilfield services providers, some of which have faced pressure on working capital and operations because of delayed collections.
-- Sheky Espejo
TotalEnergies plans to widen oil and gas exploration in Argentina: country chair
New project planned in Tierra del Fuego
Potential seen in offshore Malvinas basin
Plan to invest $4M-$5M to boost Vaca Muerta output
TotalEnergies plans to expand exploration in Argentina, including the offshore Malvinas basin, onshore conventional and Vaca Muerta oil, and dry shale gas, as part of a strategy to diversify its portfolio, the company's Country Chair, Sergio

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 21, 2026

Mengoni, said late Oct. 20. "Diversification is very important for our portfolio and I think
it's important for the country as well," by providing more sources of energy to meet domestic and export demand, Mengoni said at the Argentina Oil and Gas Patagonia Expo in Neuquén.
This strategy includes a $1.5 billion project, dubbed Alberdi, to drill three to five new wells that will connect to existing offshore platforms in the southernmost province of Tierra del Fuego, where TotalEnergies is a leading gas producer.
"This is going to produce a lot of conventional gas for the country," Mengoni said.
He said this is "a good complement" to Vaca Muerta, a huge shale play in northern Patagonia that is driving Argentina's oil and gas production growth.
As part of this diversification, TotalEnergies also plans to drill three to five onshore wells in Tierra del Fuego for both oil and gas, Mengoni added.
"It is very promising," Mengoni said of its potential. Mengoni said there are more prospects in conventional resources around the country that merit exploration. One of these is an offshore block in the Malvinas basin near its large gas developments in Tierra del Fuego, where it is working with Norway's Equinor and Argentina's state-backed YPF. "We are very excited about this block," Mengoni said, adding that a decision will be made soon about whether to continue exploration or not.
Vaca Muerta dry gas potential These conventional projects will expand on its Vaca Muerta
investments in northern Patagonia. The company has said it plans to invest between $4 billion
and $5 billion to boost gas production in Vaca Muerta, targeting 16 million cubic meters/day of output, mainly from the Aguada Pichana Este dry gas block. It also plans to invest $5 billion to boost oil production in its San Roque block to 60,000 b/d.
Mengoni said there is potential for growth in the dry gas window of Vaca Muerta, even though most of the new projects are focused on the oil and wet gas windows.
"The country's gas demand is going to increase by a lot," he said, pointing out that gas now provides half of the national energy demand. "This means that dry gas is always going to be needed to complement the energy mix."
Argentina consumes 140 million cu m/d of gas on average, exporting some 10 million cu m/d to neighboring countries. There are projects to increase exports, including under-construction and proposed LNG export facilities with up to 24 million metric tons/year of capacity, or 90 million cu m/d.
Dry gas is considered a better backup for meeting demand than the associated gas produced with oil, which is targeted for supplying the LNG export facilities. Vaca Muerta is largely a dry gas play, with 258 trillion cubic feet of its total 300 Tcf in gas resources in the gas window, according to the Argentina Oil and Gas Institute, an industry group.

Regional gas exports There is also potential for increasing gas shipments to the
region, Mengoni said. "The regional market is a very important pillar for continuing with this diversification and it allows us to reach markets that need gas."
He said the pockets for new growth are Brazil and northern Chile, as well as northern Argentina, which is undersupplied. "It is going to grow," Mengoni said of the regional exports.

Chevron betting on Argentina's Vaca Muerta for oil output growth: country head
Targets 40,000 b/d output in 2026 in Vaca Muerta
US firm partners with YPF in another block
Improving competitiveness key for growth
Chevron is betting on Vaca Muerta for oil production growth in its global portfolio, as a faster-than-expected increase in output from the Argentinian shale play grows and tax incentives make it a good investment, the company's Country Head Ana Simonato said late Oct. 20.
"Argentina is very strongly positioned" in the US-based company's global unconventional oil portfolio "because the conditions are there for long-term investments," Simonato said at the Argentina Oil and Gas Patagonia Expo in Neuquén.
In the middle of 2026, Chevron filed for tax incentives in Argentina for a project worth more than $10 billion to develop Vaca Muerta shale oil in the El Trapial Este block, north of the play. This will build on a long-term joint venture with Argentina's state-backed YPF to develop Loma Campana, now the country's largest oil source, with an output of 92,000 barrels/day.
The challenge for the company is that El Trapial Este is among the first to expand into the north hub, while most large developments, such as Loma Campana, are in the centraleastern hub.
Simonato said the north hub has less infrastructure and fewer services and it is more isolated than the central-eastern hub. This means that Chevron and other companies expanding there, including YPF and Tecpetrol, will have to work with midstream companies building oil and natural gas takeaway capacity.
Even so, Chevron's production growth in Vaca Muerta has been promising so far, encouraging the application for tax incentives for El Trapial Este, Simonato said.
Chevron reached an output target of 30,000 b/d in Vaca Muerta sooner than expected last year, and this year it is aiming for 40,000 b/d, she said. When factoring in its non-operated output, the company is producing 100,000 b/d in Vaca Muerta, Simonato added.
That will add production to Chevron's global unconventional portfolio, now producing a total of 1.7 million b/d, she said.

Tax incentives Looking ahead, she said the tax incentives -- 30 years of tax

Meanwhile, Strait of Hormuz vessel traffic dropped for a third day to 16 ships on Oct. 20, including three VLCCs, according to an Oct. 21 report by S&P Global Commodities at Sea.

REFINERY NEWS: ExxonMobil's Joliet reports second unit upset; Chicago CBOB rises 8%
Refinery: Joliet in Channahon, Illinois
Owner: ExxonMobil
Overall capacity: 267,000 b/d
Units affected: N/A
Duration: Oct. 20
ExxonMobil's Joliet refinery reported an ongoing flaring event beginning at 1:50 am on Oct. 20 that resulted in sulfur dioxide emissions, according to an Illinois Emergency Management Agency filing.
In the filing, the company attributed the release to a unit upset. The filing did not identify the affected processing unit, emission quantity, or production impacts, and indicated the event was ongoing when reported at about 5:30 am Oct. 20.
"We're working to resolve an operational issue. The safety of the community and our workforce remains our primary focus. The flaring that occurred on October 18 is unrelated to the operational issue that has resulted in the current flaring," a company spokesperson said in an Oct. 21 email.
The filing was the second made by the facility this week. In Chicago, refined product strength was driven by market talk about a refinery outage and tightening gasoline and diesel availability, according to some market participants. "The rumor mill has trouble down at Exxon Joliet," an Oct. 20 note from ADMO Energy said. "While I don't know, this would be consistent with what I am seeing out there." Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed Chicago CBOB at a 35-cent/gallon premium to NYMEX RBOB futures on Oct. 20, up from a 1.75-cent/gal premium on Oct. 19, after the first IEMA filing of the week on Oct. 18 by ExxonMobil reporting a problem at Joliet flipped the differential into positive territory. During this period, the flat price rose to $3.46/gal on Oct. 20 from $3.2089/gal a day earlier, according to Platts assessments. Chicago ULSD rose from a 31-cent/gal discount to NYMEX ULSD futures on Oct. 19 to a 5-cent/gal discount on Oct. 20, with the flat price rising to $4.875/gal on Oct. 20 from $4.6101/gal on Oct. 19, Platts assessments showed.

Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

39

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 21, 2026

launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts will also use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce

the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments during the month of loading.
In view of the changes, Platts is no longer updating the codes reflecting Upper Zakum, Das Blend and Umm Lulu OSP differentials against Murban, as well as the codes for all four grades, including Murban, calculated against the monthly average of Platts cash Dubai 1st Line assessment in the month of trade.
The following codes will no longer be updated from October:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

Diff to Dubai AOSPA00 AOSPB00 AOSPC00 AOSPD00

Diff to Murban OSP
AOSPE00 AOSPF00 AOSPG00

ADNOC's OSPs are published in the Platts price database and on Platts Global Alert page 1051.

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.

The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

Assessment FOB Middle East Bitumen VG30 FOB Middle East Bitumen VG40

Quantity

Laycan

2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 10-30 days forward

UoM $/mt

2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 10-30 days forward $/mt

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the proposed standard.
The assessments will reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.
This follows a proposal published Sept. 14 and available here. Please send any questions, comments or feedback to asia_products@spglobal.com, with a copy to pricegroup@spglobal.com. For written comments, please provide a clear indication if they are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make those not marked as confidential available upon request.

Platts proposes to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of the month, from Dec. 1, 2026.
The proposed change, which follows Platts review of historical data sets, would align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of its ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward value of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under their price database codes as below:

Platts revises measurement units, descriptions for FO Licht ethanol prices
The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their units of measurement and descriptions updated, as summarized below.
Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.

Hectoliter Pure Alcohol (HPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One HLTPA equals 1 hectoliter (100 liters) of pure alcohol. Platts proposes to launch weighted indexes for Chicago CBOB, ULSD
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to launch Chicago basket indexes for gasoline and diesel from March 2, incorporating the independent assessments for the major pipelines and Buckeye Complex and weighting them by trading volume, as well as adding a new, separate assessment for Chicago West Shore and Badger pipeline ULSD.
Feedback from market participants has indicated the need for Chicago-weighted basket indexes that reflect recent volumes traded on the Badger, West Shore, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex.
The proposed weightings would be calculated by tracking recent trades across the West Shore, Badger, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex. Total reported trades would be compiled on a cyclical basis, assuming a minimum volume of 10,000 barrels where not specified. These trades

will then be sorted by pipeline to determine the percentage contribution of each component.
The weighted index would be calculated from the average of the differentials to NYMEX futures for the West Shore/Badger pipelines, Wolverine pipeline and BCX assessments, adjusted by their corresponding weightings.
For example, if Platts is assessing Cycle 2 for Chicago ULSD and traded volumes from the previous cycles came out to 20% West Shore and Badger, 30% Wolverine and 50% Buckeye Complex and the pipeline differentials were assessed at plus 2 cents/gal, plus 3 cents/gal and plus 3.50 cents/gal, respectively, the index basket differential would be plus 3.05 cents/gal.
Platts' current assessments for Chicago CBOB and ULSD are as follows:

Symbol AATHA00 AGCWC00 ACBAE00 AAREL00 ACBAA00 AGCWA00

Name ULSD Chicago pipeline ULSD Chicago Wolverine Pipeline ULSD Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline Gasoline CBOB Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago Wolverine Pipeline

The proposed Chicago basket indexes would be as follows:
Name Gasoline CBOB Chicago Basket ULSD Chicago Basket
Platts is concurrently seeking feedback on how to incorporate West Shore and Badger into the weighted index when the 1 psi waiver is in place during the summer. More information on how Platts will adjust the West Shore and Badger assessments in accordance with the 1 psi waiver can be found here.
Additionally, Platts seeks feedback on the naming conventions for the proposal. Platts proposes to amend the assessment name from Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline to Gasoline CBOB West Shore and Badger pipeline to better represent the products assessed. This change follows the Feb. 3, 2025, removal of the Wolverine Pipeline from the basket underpinning the generic Chicago assessment due to the anticipated removal of the 1 psi waiver in multiple Midwest states and a division in RVP.
Platts is also proposing to launch a separate West Shore/ Badger assessment for Chicago ULSD; this would not replace the existing generic assessment, which would remain unchanged. The name of the new assessment would be ULSD Chicago West Shore and Badger Pipeline.
The indexes would appear on PGA pages 350, 351, 154, 24, 482, 410 and 411, as well as in US Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, and on Platts Connect.
Please send all feedback and comments by Jan. 23 to americas_products@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

Platts invites feedback on US gasoline RVP calendar for 2026 As part of its commitment to open and transparent pricing
and product specifications, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, would like to invite feedback regarding publishing dates and/or cycles for 2026 US gasoline RVP shifts.
The 2026 calendar largely follows 2025 norms, with no notable deviations from prior years. The calendar will be updated throughout the year as more information becomes available.
The shift dates are based on relevant pipeline schedules, historical information, and feedback from market participants.
US Gulf Coast shifts are based on the Colonial Pipeline schedule. US Atlantic Coast shifts are based on Buckeye Pipeline and Colonial Pipeline calendars.
On the West Coast, Los Angeles, San Francisco and Arizona RVP changes are based on Kinder Morgan's SFPP schedule, while Portland and Seattle shifts are based on Olympic

Pipeline's calendar. In the Midwest, Group 3 shifts are based on the ONEOK
Pipeline schedule, while Chicago shifts are based on Explorer Pipeline and Buckeye Complex schedules.
The US Environmental Protection Agency removed a waiver in eight Midwestern states that allowed for an additional 1 psi during the summer. Platts will adjust the RVP Calendar for Chicago CBOB in accordance with the implementation of the waiver. More information can be found here.
For questions or comments, please contact americas_products@spglobal.com with a cc to pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

2-DAY TRAINING
Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.
